## Goal
Find out, before anything is built, why material facts are still left out of the note with no warning after 0.3.7, and what else still fails the validation gate on the seven seen synthetic sets. The plan re-transcribes the sets, reproduces 0.3.7's result, classifies every remaining failure by cause, measures on the practitioner's kept recordings where the 0.3.7 rules act, and prints report-only numbers for three candidate rules. It ends in the practitioner's decision: build a chosen form (by an amendment to this plan), hand the map to the next plan, or close with no change. No product code changes here; shadow mode stays on.

## Planning Extraction Summary

**Workflow Schema:** v22

**Executor tier:** entirely premium — planned on Fable 5.2; executor Opus-class; tier-gap dosing applied (design decisions, the cause-class list with its fixed precedence, the candidate definitions, per-task acceptance criteria locked)

### Agreed Scope (Build Now)
- A fresh scratch library with two premises: Premise 1 proved before any text-level number is read, Premise 2 before any audio-level number the scratch computes (D2).
- The seven seen sets rebuilt and re-transcribed, the two key sets twice; 0.3.7's result reproduced on the new transcripts in the gate configuration, with a noise floor (D3, D4).
- The gate-failure map: every failure on the seven sets given exactly one cause from a closed list (D5).
- Report-only numbers for three candidates — the follow-on line, the off-record guard, the later-reply flag — at text level and at audio level with suggestions off (D6, D7, D8). No threshold, no exit named GATE-CAPABLE or IMPROVEMENT, no gate-configuration run of a candidate.
- Real speech: a numbers-only attribution measurement on the practitioner's kept recordings, run by the practitioner (D9).
- Records on every exit, the 0.3.7 harm (`h6-09/f1`) registered, and the exit decision (D10, D11).

### Deferred — Actionable Later
- **Building any candidate (the follow-on line, the off-record guard, the later-reply flag, or a cause-driven rule the map points to).**
  - Why deferred: the practitioner's "Diagnosis first (Recommended)" (2026-11-18, `/review-plan`). The draft's improvement exit could not be met on its own numbers, and half the silent facts are lost on the audio for causes not yet seen.
  - Intended future outcome: a deterministic routing or flag change whose form and thresholds are chosen from the map's causes.
  - Relevant files / subsystems: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note.py` (`generate_sections`), `ui/models.py` (`replies_after`), `validation.py` (`word_info`), the tests and documents listed in Key Findings ("If a build is chosen").
  - Dependencies / prerequisites: Task 5.3's decision; the Key Findings' "If a build is chosen" inventory.
  - Recommended next action: on a "build" decision, an amendment to THIS plan adding the build phases, hardened by a scoped `/review-plan` before any of them runs.
  - Risk if deferred: correctness: the 12 silent facts stay silent, and the gate stays unmet for that residue.
  - Revisit by: Task 5.3.
- **The shipped off-record phrase list.**
  - Why deferred: a shipped list is a new frozen vocabulary and needs the isolated-session method (validation-run7 D2's precedent). This plan uses a declared diagnostic list that never ships (D7).
  - Intended future outcome: a ratified, frozen list with exact pins, if a build uses the guard.
  - Relevant files / subsystems: `note.py`, `desktop/tests/test_vocabulary.py`.
  - Dependencies / prerequisites: a build decision that includes the guard.
  - Recommended next action: in the build amendment — a brief with rules and source families only, the isolated `claude -p` session's log kept, a mechanical acceptance scratch with its record, a near-miss and an apostrophe pin per phrase.
  - Risk if deferred: minor.
  - Revisit by: Task 5.3.

### Key Design Decisions
- D1 — diagnosis first: no product code, no tracked tool, no threshold; the plan ends at a decision. Still applies to follow-up work: Yes.
- D4 — the baseline is reproduced and a noise floor measured before any difference is read. Still applies: Yes.
- D5 — a closed cause list with a fixed precedence; "unknown" is counted, never forced. Still applies: Yes.
- D6–D8 — the candidates are report-only, with raw silent facts printed beside every credit and every harm column. Still applies: Yes.
- (Full text, reasons and rejected alternatives: Design Decisions.)

## Key Findings
All verified on 2026-11-18 at `a18e88b` (`main`, clean; version fields 0.4.0). `N` = `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note.py`, `M` = `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/models.py`, `V` = `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/validation.py`, `L` = `.cursor/loops/` (git-ignored), `AFQ` = `.cursor/plans/plan-answers-follow-question.md`.

### Files / Symbols Involved
- **The router.** `ExtractiveNoteProvider.generate_sections` (`N:2350-2453`). Per utterance, first match: blank → transparent (`N:2373`); a cue routes it (`N:2376`); the answer hop (`N:2392`); the bounded carry (`N:2407`); dropped (`N:2434`). A drop clears only `prev_cued` and `window_key`; `prev_key` and `prev_speaker` survive it. The hop, the carry and the drop all clear `prev_cued`, so `prev_cued` is true only when the immediately previous non-transparent line was cue-routed.
- **Helpers.** `_answer_is_clean` (`N:2339`: no suspect word, not a question, no cue of either layer), `_opens_answer_window` (`N:2316`), guard G `_question_routes_alone` (`N:2292`, needs both cue layers and `section_keys`), `any_cue_matches` (`N:1973`), `spoken_by_confirmed_clinician` (`N:1688`; false for every line when no clinician is confirmed, so "not the clinician" needs `clinician is not None` beside it), `CLINICIAN_OWNED_SECTIONS` (`N:170`), `whole_utterance_assertion` (`N:2006`), `CORRECTION_MARKERS` (`N:1801`), `content_tokens` and `_contains_phrase` (`N:259`, `N:1656`), `words_hold_suspect` (`transcription.py:403`). `consultation_phrases.py:75` imports `note`; `note.py` does not import it.
- **The flag.** `_reply_turn` (`M:2858`), `_added_only_clinician_lines` (`M:2892`), `replies_after(note, document)` (`M:2915`), `flagged_segments` (`M:2961`, called at `ui/note.py:1498`). `_reply_turn(index + 1)` returns nothing when the clinician's carried piece follows the question (`M:2871-2873`).
- **The harness.** `encounter_metrics` (`V:1971-2433`); the reply credit `replies_after(note, document)` with no keyword (`V:2122`); `word_info` (`V:2155-2192`: on a `high_risk_omission` segment that gets neither the whole-run credit nor the reply credit, only the flag token itself is credited, `V:2170`, `V:2181`); `FactOutcome` (`V:861-911`: `verdict`, `material`, `warned`, `silent_omission`; the review-mark flag is internal, surfacing only through `residue_r1_marked`, `V:1003`); R1 (`V:2332-2341`: a silent fact the reference reading gets right is R1 and leaves the adjusted silent count, `V:991`); `_encounter_failures` (`V:2885-2933`: `harness_error` or `role_unresolved` alone when the encounter did not measure, `V:2897`; otherwise `unsupported_clinical_line`, `checker_failure`, `material_wrong`, `silent_omission`, `uncertainty_not_surfaced`, `word_error_rate`, `prose_unavailable`, `suggestions_unavailable`); the injection point `HarnessInputs.note_provider_factory` (`V:3197`); `validation.replies_after` and `_omission_flag_token` are module globals (`V:216`, `V:144`) and can be patched in scratch.

So a dropped "don't write this down" line is already `[flagged]`.

### Codebase Integration Notes
- **Where the 12 silent facts sit** (the scripts, and `L/afq-out/text/<set>/V4g.json`). Silent on the script's own words: `h2-07/f4,f5` (the patient's second line after their own cue-routed reply, which closed the window), `h6-16/f5` ("Um, nine, …", a correction with an unlisted opener after the patient's own cue-routed line; the line holds a number and is already `[flagged]`, so it is "silent" only because the credit is per word), `h6-05/f2` (volunteered after a left-out clinician statement; also already flagged through a number), `h6-06/f5` (a reply to a left-out instruction), `h2-04/f6` (after a partner's line). Lost only on the audio: `h2-07/f3` (same script line as `f2`, which is not silent — probably a split piece), `h6-08/f0,f1` and `h6-22/f0` (credited by the reply flag at text level), `h6-21/f4` (joined at text level), `h6-15/f5` (an overlapped line the recogniser returned with no words, `docs/testing/validation-harness.md:413`). The saved transcripts went with `C:\afq-sets\` (deleted 2026-11-17), so the audio causes are not observable yet.
- **An unrecorded 0.3.7 harm.** `h6-09/f1` is `omitted` in `none-rerun.json` and `wrong` in `product.json`: the hop joined segments and placed a misheard piece. 0.3.7's harm thresholds (AFQ D8) counted only quoted `absent` facts. The register has only F-160 for `h6-09` (`docs/pilot/findings-register.md:261`).
- **Same-commit transcription drift is real** (AGENTS.md, Last Session 2026-11-10: `heldout-2` moved on 7 of 22 encounters on one re-run). The 12 facts belong to transcripts made at `22feec4`.
- **What the seen sets cannot show.** No script has a next patient speaking two lines in a row (every such turn is one line; the four two-line cases are split by a clinician line), while 9 of the 50 next-patient lines carry a cue. So at text level "no next-patient line newly quoted" is clean by absence for any follow-on form; at audio level it can be exercised where the recogniser splits a next-patient line after its cue-bearing piece, or where a next patient's unsplit, uncued line carries the same speaker label as a cue-routed line just before it (a label merge, no split needed — corrected 2026-11-18, round 7 PR-LOW-101). Voices (corrected 2026-11-18, round 7 PR-LOW-102): 121 of the 183 scripts use two voice slots and 62 use three or more (a next patient or another person, e.g. `h6-16`: clinician 0, patient 2, next_patient 1), so a third voice merged into another's label, or a clinician fragment labelled as the patient (the Known Issues' real-speech observations, 2026-10-03 and 2026-11-17), can arise in replay; whether it does is observed per segment by Task 2.3's trace (D5's role mapping), not assumed. A parent, carer or interpreter in a real room is not in these sets.
- **Off-record lines in the sets.** One patient line (`hld-17` line 2, a material `absent` fact). About a dozen clinician instructions, most with words between the phrase's parts ("leave the bit about the boss out of the note", `h5-08`), which a contiguous phrase cannot match. (Corrected 2026-11-18, round 8 PR-LOW-202: `h4-17`'s "don't put the wedding in the letter" was named here as a second such example, but the contiguous phrase "don't put" matches it — `ffl-text-5.txt`, "instruction lines the list matches: h4-17:L5"; the list matches 1 of the 12 instruction lines, Task 1.2.) `h4-06` line 3 ("leave breaststroke kick out for now") is a material `present` fact: a collision risk for any list. The line after a request (`syn-31` line 5) is already protected, because the drop closes the window and the chain.
- **Pins a later build would move** (found by running the suite with in-memory patches during review; nothing was written): `test_note.py` `TestBoundedCarry` (14), `TestAnswerHop` (2, plus the two "don't write this down" tests at `:3400` and `:3411`, and the carry's pinned residue at `:3254`), `test_note_matrix.py` cells `ex-numbers-meds-doses` and `ex-comma-then-instruction` (`:649`), `test_note_check.py` `_PREDICTED_UNSURE_DIVERGENCES_BY_SET` (`:2814`, an `h6-15` entry), `test_note.py` `_PREDICTED_CORRECTION_DIVERGENCES_BY_SET` (`:3503`), `test_validation_scripts.py` `_hop_joined_lines` (`:220`), `test_validation_metrics.py` `TestReplyCredit` (4, among them `test_an_added_question_keeps_its_reply_flagged_but_never_credited`), `test_vocabulary.py` (a new pin). No report byte pin, `_HELD_OUT_DOCUMENTS_SHA256`, `test_replay_kept.py`, `test_pilot_docs.py` or UI pin moved.

A scoped executor leg cannot read outside the repository; the composer copies what it needs into `L/`. `docs/testing/validation-harness.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/security/threat-model.md`, `docs/security/data-flow-map.md` and the plans are CRLF in the working tree. `TestHarnessDocument` (`desktop/tests/test_validation_harness.py:2208-2213`) reads table rows that start with a back-ticked cell inside the rule sections, so a new table goes outside them. ## Planned Workflow Summary

### Flow 1 — Scratch and premises (the agent)
- A fresh scratch library re-implements the router with a reason code per line, and proves it equals the shipped provider before any candidate is switched on. The three candidates are then read at text level (each script's own words).

### Flow 2 — Audio (the agent, on the practitioner's "start now")
- The seven sets are rebuilt outside the repository and transcribed once, the two key sets twice. The shipped product runs over them in the gate configuration; its result is compared fact by fact with 0.3.7's saved outputs, and the difference between two takes of one set gives the noise floor.

### Flow 3 — The map (the agent)
- Every failure on the seven sets gets one cause from the closed list. The candidates are read at audio level with suggestions off, each beside its harm columns.

### Flow 4 — Real speech (the practitioner)
- A reviewed, hash-frozen, numbers-only script runs over the kept recordings from a normal terminal with both builds closed, and prints where the 0.3.7 rules act and where the candidates would. The practitioner pastes the numbers.

### Flow 5 — Records and the decision (both)
- The map, the candidate tables and the 0.3.7 harm go into the harness document and the findings register. The practitioner chooses: build a form (an amendment to this plan), hand the map on, or close with no change. The plan closes on every exit.

## Design Decisions
- **D1 — Diagnosis first; the plan ends at a decision.** Nothing under `desktop/src/`, `scripts/`, `extension/` or `validation/` changes. No threshold is set and no exit is named GATE-CAPABLE or IMPROVEMENT: the improvement bar the draft copied from 0.3.7 ("silent facts at least halved on each set") needs 8 → 4 on `heldout-6`, where the candidates reach one fact at text level.
  Alternatives considered: the draft's candidate grid, an 8-hour gate-configuration run and build phases in one plan (commits a version to an exit its own arithmetic cannot reach); a diagnosis-only plan with no candidate numbers (the text-level numbers are cheap and the practitioner asked for them).
- **D2 — Scratch only, fresh, with two premises.** `L/ffl_lib.py` copies the replay, capture, alignment and counter helpers from `L/afq_lib.py` (which stays untouched as 0.3.7's method record) and re-implements the shipped loop — hop included — in a provider subclass injected through `HarnessInputs.note_provider_factory`, emitting one reason code per utterance (cued, joined, carried, followed, refused-by-guard, dropped, each with the test that decided it). "Equals" means, per encounter: the whole note (every assertion, its coordinates, section key and order), the check warnings, the `EncounterMetrics`, the reference reading and the credit sets (the comparison `afq-measure.py:600-612` made). Premise 1 (text level): with every candidate off it equals the shipped provider on all seven sets, 0 differences. Premise 2 (replay): over every saved transcript it equals the shipped provider, 0 differences — text level never exercised switch S, a carry after a join, blank segments or label disagreement (`AFQ:1322`). A difference is a finding against the scratch. Premise 1 comes before any text-level number and Premise 2 before any audio-level number the scratch computes (the traces, the map, the candidates). Task 2.2's baseline is the shipped product's own run with no scratch provider, so it does not wait for Premise 2. Premise 2 runs straight after transcription (Task 2.1) and again over a retry take before that take is used; Task 1.2's tables are provisional until Premise 2 passes, and are regenerated after it.
  Alternatives considered: reusing `afq_lib.HopProvider` (it predates the product's hop and is a method record); a harness flag (changes shipped code before a decision).
- **D3 — Where things live.** Built sets `C:\ffl-sets\<set>`; pickled transcripts `C:\ffl-sets\docs\<set>` (take 1) and `C:\ffl-sets\docs-t2\<set>` (take 2, `heldout-2` and `heldout-6` only) and `C:\ffl-sets\docs-t3\<set>` (a retry take, only where D4 asks for one; `afq-transcribe.py:212` skips an existing pickle, so every take has its own folder and none is overwritten or deleted); logs, traces and tables under `L/ffl-out/` [as built (round 19 PR-LOW-1006, 2026-11-19): the run logs sit directly under `L/` (`ffl-chain.out`, `ffl-c<N>-*.txt`, `ffl-premise2-*.txt`, `ffl-baseline-*.txt`, `ffl-docs-*.txt`, …); `L/ffl-out/` holds the structured outputs — the summaries, the text projections, the baseline payloads and the traces]. All synthetic. The composer runs the builds and transcriptions and copies into `L/` whatever a scoped leg must read. The practitioner deletes `C:\ffl-sets\` at the close.
- **D4 — Reproduce first, then a noise floor.** `L/afq-transcribe.py` is reused unchanged. The shipped product (no scratch provider) runs in the gate configuration (`--suggestions confirm-all --prose`, rule v6, the validation config) over take 1 of all seven sets and take 2 of the two. The result is saved per fact as index, material, verdict, warned, the adjusted silent flag (`silent_omission_v2`), the raw silent flag and the residue flags, once with the reply credit and once without. Per fact, take 1 is compared with `L/afq-out/built/<set>/product.json` on its five fields, `QT` against the default credit and `shipped` against the calculation without the reply credit. Fixed before any run: take 1 is ALWAYS this plan's baseline of record, whatever it shows; no take is chosen by its result. Where a set's take 1 differs from the archive, one retry take is made (`docs-t3`) and used only to mark each differing fact "also on the retry" or "not on the retry" (the 2026-11-10 lesson); the differences are recorded by name, fact by fact. The noise floors are measured on this 0.3.7 baseline, per set and per measure, between two takes of the set: takes 1 and 2 for `heldout-2` and `heldout-6`, and take 1 and the retry for any other set that has one. The fact-level floor is the number of material facts whose reported verdict, or whose RAW silent flag under the default credit (with the reply credit), differs between the two takes; it is the floor for the raw-silent column, and the same count taken without the reply credit is the floor for that column. Every other LEVEL column of D8 (R1, H6) has its own floor: the absolute difference of the baseline's value in that column between the two takes. A column that is itself a difference from the baseline (facts newly placed, lines added, H1–H5, H7) has no floor: it is printed for each take side by side. For a set with no second take each measure takes the largest floor measured for it on any set. A candidate's difference in a level column is printed as "within noise" only when it is no larger than that column's floor for that set; no floor is carried from one measure to another.
  Alternatives considered: trusting the saved outputs (their transcripts are gone); transcribing every set twice (another 1 hour 44 minutes for sets that hold none of the 12).
- **D5 — The cause list is closed, mechanical and ordered.** Each failure gets the first class whose test holds; none holding is `unknown`, which is counted and listed, never forced. The tests read only the trace (Task 2.3's schema). A failure is identified by (encounter, fact index or rule code, predicate), the predicate one of silent, wrong, wrongly present or the rule code; a fact that is wrong on one equal-cost alignment and silent on another has one row per predicate. A judgement test reads the fact's captured end states (Task 2.2): it holds when it holds on every end state, except W5, which holds when some end state judges the fact correct. A segment test (which segment, which label, which note line) reads the fact's segment set — the union over the optimal alignments (`fact_segments`, `afq_lib.py:927`); the walk merges equal states and keeps no per-alignment history (`V:1433`), so alignments are not listed one by one — and holds only when it holds for every segment of the set; a set whose segments disagree fails the test and the row is marked "mixed". Each row also carries "silent at text level too: yes or no" as a column, not a class. Roles and labels: a segment's true role is the role of the script lines aligned to it (lines of two roles give it none); a label's role is the role holding the duration-weighted majority of that label's seconds on the timed label track, computed for every label the way `clinician_speaker` computes the clinician's (`V:3044-3081`) with the same tie rule (`ROLE_TIE_SECONDS`), and a tied label has no role. A segment is "mislabelled" when it has a true role and its label has no role or another one. Two roles under one label (three voices, two labels) make every segment of the minority role mislabelled. Separately, the router-facing test: the trace records the one confirmed clinician label (`clinician_speaker`'s choice, `V:3044-3081`), and a segment "crosses the clinician line" when (its true role is the clinician) differs from (its label is the confirmed clinician label) — the test `N:1688` applies. It is recorded for every fact segment, mislabelled or not: a clinician fragment under a second clinician-majority label is correctly labelled by the majority mapping and still non-clinician to the router.
  - Silent facts, in order: **S6** the fact's script line aligned to no words, or only to suspect words; **S5** the fact segment aligns to script lines of two roles (it has no single true role); **S2** the fact segment is mislabelled or crosses the clinician line (the role mapping above); **S1** the fact sits in a later piece of a script line whose first piece is in the note; **S4** a window was open and was closed by the replier's own cue-routed line before the fact's line (at text level or at audio); **S7** the line is cue-routed at text level and its segment is not (the cue was misheard); **S3** at text level a window opened before it, at audio the question segment is not interrogative or fails guard G; **S8** warned at text level and not at audio (the flag's anchor or run was lost); **S9** the fallback: silent at text level too, with none of the causes above (no rule reaches it on the script's own words). Expected before the classifier runs, from the scripts: `h2-07/f4`, `h2-07/f5` and `h6-16/f5` are S4; `h6-05/f2`, `h6-06/f5` and `h2-04/f6` are S9. A different result is recorded as a finding against the classifier or against this reading, never edited away.
  - Wrong facts, in order: **W5** some equal-cost alignment judges it correct; **W6** on a line added by the suggestion pass; **W4** on a hop-joined or carried line; **W3** only part of the script line is quoted (a split); **W2** on a line with a review mark; **W1** on a cue-routed line with no mark.
  - Wrongly present facts, in order: **N3** on a line added by the suggestion pass; **N4** the segment is mislabelled or crosses the clinician line (the role mapping above; a segment aligned to lines of two roles has no true role and is not counted); **N1** in the reference tail with no boundary found; **N2** in the tail with the boundary found late; **P4** quoted by the hop or the carry; **P2** quoted by its own cue.
  - Other encounter failures, by their rule code: **U** `unsupported_clinical_line`, **C** `checker_failure`, **D** `uncertainty_not_surfaced` (split by R2 tone-only or not), **E** `word_error_rate`, **X** `prose_unavailable` / `suggestions_unavailable`, **Z** `harness_error`, **R** `role_unresolved` (the last two are returned alone, for an encounter that did not measure). Set level: the R1, R2 and R3 counts against rule v6's ceilings.
  Alternatives considered: free-text reasons (not countable, not repeatable); letting the executor add classes (the list could be fitted to the answer wanted).
- **D6 — The candidates, report-only.** Each is a switch on D2's provider or on a patched `replies_after`; none is a proposal to ship as written.
  - **A1, the follow-on line.** An utterance U that no cue routes, the hop does not join and the carry does not take follows the section of the immediately previous non-transparent utterance P when: P was cue-routed (`prev_cued`); P and U have the same speaker; a clinician is confirmed and that speaker is not the clinician; P holds no suspect word; P's section is not clinician-owned; U passes `_answer_is_clean`. One line only: a followed line clears `prev_cued` and opens no window.
  - **A1s, the safe form.** A1, and U would draw no `high_risk_omission` if left out — so the form never removes a `high_risk_omission` warning (the `h6-09/f1` class). It can still remove a REPLY flag: a line it places is covered, and `_reply_turn` marks only wholly uncovered lines (`M:2879-2887`); D8 counts those.
  - **Whole-turn reach.** One diagnostic count per set: the silent facts a whole-turn form of A1 would additionally reach, printed under the name "the rejected turn-level form".
  - **F, the later-reply flag (flag only).** A third source for the chooser's mark: the reply turn after each confirmed-clinician question that is in the note by any assertion, starting after the clinician's contiguous in-note run. Printed twice: as marks only (no harness credit), and with the credit, the second named as a reversal of AFQ D4.
  - **C0.** One metric-only number: the silent facts that sit on a non-clinician line already carrying a `high_risk_omission`, which today's per-word credit leaves "silent" though the line is flagged.
- **D7 — The off-record guard uses a DIAGNOSTIC list that never ships.** `holds_off_record` in the scratch only: contiguous whole-token phrases over `content_tokens` of every word, suspect ones included, gating the hop, the carry and A1 (a refused line takes the drop path). The list, declared here and used as written: "don't write", "do not write", "don't record", "do not record", "don't put", "do not put", "off the record", "not for the notes", "between you and me", "keep that out", "leave that out". Measured as **G0** (0.3.7 plus the guard only — expected 0 note differences on all seven sets, said beforehand) and beside A1 and A1s. Printed with it: the lines it refuses, by role; the refused lines that hold a `present` fact (new silent omissions); the refused lines Check 4 still flags; the clinician instruction lines it does NOT match.
  Alternatives considered: the isolated-session method now (heavy for a report-only number; it is the Deferred item); fitting the list to the sets (one patient line cannot select a list).
- **D8 — Every table prints the cost beside the reach.** Per set and per candidate: raw silent facts with and without the reply credit; R1 as the rule counts it — `class_counts`' total against the set's ceiling (`V:2995-3030`: silent and marked R1 together on six sets; silent only on `heldout-6`, whose marked R1 is reported beside it) — with R1-silent and R1-marked also printed separately (a rule that grows the reference reading turns audio losses into R1 without fixing them); facts newly placed; lines added; **H1** material `absent` facts quoted on a joined, carried or followed line; **H2** non-material ones; **H3** material facts `omitted` in the baseline and `wrong` in the variant, split by whether the line carries a review mark (computed in scratch from the line marks, since `FactOutcome` has no such field); **H4** next-patient lines newly quoted — printed with the standing note that at text level these sets cannot test it, and at audio only through a split piece or a speaker-label merge (Key Findings; the label merge added 2026-11-18, round 7 PR-LOW-101 — the note's wording, not H4's definition); **H5** new checker failures and error-grade warnings, gross; **H6** unsupported clinical lines; **H7** `high_risk_omission` warnings removed, reply flags removed (lines in the baseline's reply-source set — `replies_after(note, document, added_questions=True)`, `M:2915` — that the candidate quotes; a line flagged only by an omission anchor or a flagged run, `M:2975`, is counted under the warnings removed, not here), extra flagged lines per encounter, must-stay-out lines newly flagged. Differences in a level column within D4's matching floor are marked; difference columns are printed per take (D4).
- **D9 — Real speech is described, not judged.** `L/ffl-measure-kept.py`, modelled on `L/afq-measure-kept.py`, following the replay tool's rules (`docs/testing/kept-recordings.md`: a normal terminal, both builds closed, the crash-report exclusion check, the single-instance lock, refusals before anything is decrypted). It prints numbers only, per recording and in total: whether the clinician was enrolled; clinician-labelled lines; of those, questions; cue-routed questions; windows opened (guard G passed); lines the hop joined; replies flagged; and for the candidates, lines A1 and A1s would follow, lines the diagnostic guard would refuse, extra marks from F. Two passes, as 0.3.7 ran them: the validation cue file, then the installed app's own. Before the practitioner runs it: a review, a dry run over the synthetic transcripts, the refusal smoke, and the SHA-256 of the script and of `ffl_lib.py` recorded on the task (Task 4.1 holds the re-freeze rule for a later change). A pass counts only if it ends `COMPLETE`. No threshold is read here.
- **D10 — Records.** In `docs/testing/validation-harness.md`, a new section "Gate-failure map (2026-11)" placed outside the rule sections: the method, the baseline reproduction and noise floor, the cause table, the candidate tables, the real-speech totals, the decision. Synthetic encounter ids only. The same document's "Answer-hop simulation" section gains one dated correction line for `h6-09/f1`. `docs/pilot/findings-register.md` gains one row for that harm (Stage and vocabularies as `desktop/tests/test_pilot_docs.py` pins them; month only). `docs/testing/kept-recordings.md` (lines 7–9), the threat model and the data-flow map name the new one-off script where they name `afq-measure-kept.py`. `AFQ`'s Deferred "later lines" item and its Follow-Up Continuation Notes 1 each gain one dated reconciliation line. `AGENTS.md` and `CHANGELOG.md` as `/document` routes them.
- **D11 — Three exits at Task 5.3**, each closing the plan's own work: **build** (the practitioner names the form; the build phases are added to THIS plan by an amendment and hardened by a scoped `/review-plan`; thresholds are fixed then and declared post-hoc on seen sets; whether three enrolled kept recordings are a precondition is decided then), **hand on** (no change; the map seeds plan 2 or a cause-driven plan), **close** (no change).
- **D12 — No version, no build of record, no install; the UI refresh plan stays as it is.** Its P.3 is still open and it stays Active. This plan needs no build loop: its work is scratch, long runs and documents, so `/execute` in one session fits; if `/execute-loop` is used, its wizard is told there are two Active plans (the Custom path).

## Schema / Data Changes
None. Nothing at rest changes; no store, record, setting file or consent text is touched.

## Config / Environment / Deployment Impact
None for the product: no version, no build of record, no install; the installed app stays 0.4.0 with shadow mode on. For the runs: the installed app closed, mains power and the lid open while Task 2.1 and Task 2.2 run (about 2 hours and about 2.5 hours); about 1 GB free on `C:` for the built sets.

## Critical Constraints
- **C1 — No product change.** Until Task 5.3 nothing under `desktop/src/`, `desktop/tests/`, `scripts/`, `extension/`, `validation/` or `protocol/` is edited. The only tracked files this plan edits are the documents D10 names, this plan, and the related plans' dated lines — all in Phase 5.
- **C2 — Real recordings.** The agent never reads, plays or describes a kept recording, transcript or note. Real-speech numbers come only from the practitioner's run of D9's script. No session id, per-recording number or accent count enters a tracked file: Task 4.2's pasted output stays in the conversation and the off-repository pilot log, and the documents carry totals only.
- **C3 — Synthetic only.** `C:\ffl-sets\` and `L/ffl-out/` hold synthetic content only. The scratch's synthetic and real-speech entry points are separate files, so no real-data path shares id-printing code.
- **C4 — Frozen things stay frozen.** No cue file, vocabulary list, rule file, held-out set, prompt, consent text or `provider_name` changes. The diagnostic off-record list exists in the scratch only (D7).
- **C5 — Nothing here is a gate.** The seven sets are seen. Every number is a measurement, never a run of record; rule v6's verdict of record stays run 9's; `docs/pilot/exit-gate.md` is unchanged and the records say so.
- **C6 — The cause list and the candidate definitions are fixed by this plan.** A class or a candidate is not added, dropped or redefined after a number has been read; what does not fit is `unknown` or a recorded finding for Task 5.3.
- **C7 — Runs.** Background, the lid open; in the agent's Bash shell every Python call takes `< /dev/null` and no `python -` reads a heredoc (the practitioner's PowerShell commands of Tasks 4.1 and 4.2 take no redirection); scripts over about 1 KB are written to a file first. Tasks 2.1 and 2.2 start only on the practitioner's "start now", with the installed app closed.
- **C8 — Line endings and custody.** Every edited tracked file keeps its line ending (check `git ls-files --eol` first). Nothing is committed or pushed without the practitioner's word. Cloud sessions stay off.

- Proposed amendment: keep the archived adjusted field for the comparison, save raw silence and the residue flags beside it, and map archived `QT` to today's default credit and archived `shipped` to the no-reply-credit calculation.

#### PR-MED-002: `plan:D5`, `plan:Task 2.3` — the trace cannot support every class test
- Materiality: build-affecting (verified)
- Why it matters: W5 needs each alternative alignment's judgement (`validation.py:2257`, reduced at `:2275`); N3 and W6 need a note line's origin; a fact can be wrong on one alternative and silent on another (`validation.py:869`), so "one row per failure" is ambiguous; a segment aligned to two roles has no single true role.

- Decision: Applied 2026-11-18 (Claude Code, plan amendment). Landed in: D5 (the failure identity, the any/all rule, the text-level column), Task 2.2 (notes and outcomes saved), Task 2.3 (the trace schema), Validation. Siblings reconciled.

#### PR-MED-001: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/validation.py:2315` — the captured walk includes a second, comparison walk
- Materiality: build-affecting (verified)
- Why it matters: inside each fact's iteration `encounter_metrics` may walk every statement again with `infos_v034` (conditional, short-circuited by `any`), calling the same `_fact_end_states` and `_judge`; matched by call order alone, those calls would be attributed to the wrong fact or statement and break the parity line and the classes.
- Proposed amendment: tag each captured call by metrics invocation and walk kind, and keep the comparison walk out of parity and of D5.
- Decision: Applied 2026-11-18 (Claude Code, plan amendment). Landed in: Task 2.2 (each captured call tagged by invocation and walk kind; the comparison walk kept out of the parity line and of D5). Siblings reconciled.

#### PR-MED-002: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/validation.py:3063`, `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note.py:1688` — the majority mapping misses a clinician fragment under a second clinician-majority label
- Materiality: build-affecting (verified)
- Why it matters: `clinician_speaker` may find several clinician-majority labels and confirms exactly one; the router tests that one label's identity. A clinician fragment under another clinician-majority label is correctly labelled by the majority mapping and non-clinician to the router, so it escaped S2, N4 and the clinician-line column.
- Proposed amendment: record the confirmed clinician label and test `(true role is clinician) != (label is the confirmed clinician label)` directly.
- Decision: Applied 2026-11-18 (Claude Code, plan amendment). Landed in: D5 (the confirmed clinician label and the router-facing test, recorded for every fact segment; S2 and N4 widened), Task 2.3 (the trace). Siblings reconciled.

#### PR-MED-004: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/models.py:2975` — "reply flags removed" would also count other flags

### Round 7 — 2026-11-18
Source: Codex — codex gpt-6.2-astra medium — Phase 1 code peer review (stage-1.p1, peer round 1)
Status: Closed (6 applied in leg ax7, plus one composer-observed formatting follow-on applied in leg ax8; verified 2026-11-18 by the composer's re-runs `ffl-selftest-3.txt` (0 mismatches), `ffl-text-4.txt` and `ffl-text-5.txt` (errors 0, no trace mismatch, no table cell changed) and the empty-`--sets` refusal (exit 2))

Scope:

- job 1: checked the candidate-off provider against the shipped loop, including transparent blanks, speaker changes, a carried question reopening the answer window, and state retained after a drop. No routing divergence found. The copied AFQ helpers agree with their originals apart from the documented measurement adaptations. The saved Premise 1 output covers 183 encounters with zero mismatches.
- job 2: checked A1, A1s, the whole-turn continuation, all 11 guard phrases and their placement before hop/carry/follow, both F modes against the shipped reply-turn helper, and C0. No discrepancy found in when the candidate switches fire.
- job 3: checked the D8 columns, guard counts and stay-out lines against the fact walk, chooser flags and Check 4 anchors. The saved rows support the reported text-level totals. H3 can attribute a mark to the wrong occurrence on audio, and a missing candidate trace can silently suppress guard evidence.
- job 4: checked the six baseline silent fact IDs, the 37 without reply credit, the three reproduced estimates, G0’s zero note differences and the provisional label. The two saved text summaries agree after line-ending normalization. The H4 footnote overstates what audio can exercise, and the guard output omits the identity of its one matched instruction.

Findings:

- **PR-MED-101** (MED, measurement correctness, `.cursor/loops/ffl_lib.py:1026`): H3 can report a newly wrong fact as marked when the mark belongs to a different occurrence. `fact_segments` pools every occurrence and optimal alignment, while the harness takes the worst verdict. A marked correct occurrence can therefore mask an unmarked wrong one. All saved text-level H3 counts are zero. - Evidence: `on_mark = bool(facts_to_segments.get(index, frozenset()) & marked)`, versus `worst = max(judged, key=_severity)` at `validation.py:2276`. Recommendation: Fix-now - derive H3’s mark from the segment supporting each wrong occurrence and report unmarked if any wrong occurrence lacks a marked line.
- **PR-MED-102** (MED, premise verification, `.cursor/loops/ffl_lib.py:787`): A missing or empty set directory contributes zero comparisons and can still yield a successful Premise 1 result. An empty `--sets` selection bypasses every comparison. A valid partial selection also gets an estimate labelled “over the seven sets.” The saved run itself contains all seven sets. - Evidence: `for path in sorted((REPO / "validation" / set_name).glob("*.json"))`, `return 0 if total == 0 else 1` at `ffl-measure.py:185`, and `lines added over the seven sets` at `ffl-measure.py:491`. Recommendation: Fix-now - reject empty selections and empty requested sets, and label partial runs with the sets actually measured.
- **PR-MED-103** (MED, trace verification, `.cursor/loops/ffl-measure.py:221`): A missing candidate trace passes the check added in round 6. Its guard refusals then appear as zero and its joined/carried/followed segments as empty. Premise 1 already treats a missing base trace as a mismatch. - Evidence: `if ev.record is not None and not _trace_ok(ev.record):`, followed downstream by `refused = sorted(record.segments_with("refused")) if record is not None else []` at `ffl_lib.py:1151`. Recommendation: Fix-now - raise the variant-specific trace error when the record is missing or fails `_trace_ok`.
- **PR-LOW-101** (LOW, interpretation, `.cursor/loops/ffl-measure.py:447`): The H4 footnote rules out an unsplit audio path that A1 allows. For example, a cue-routed clinician line attributed to the patient can precede an unsplit, uncued next-patient line carrying the same observed speaker label. At least one named script assigns three distinct voice slots in the same encounter. Both modes refuse an empty selection or empty requested set. A missing trace raises a variant-specific error. The H4 footnote covers label merges. The 183 synthetic scripts confirm 121 with two distinct voice slots and 62 with three. The output names `h4-17:L5` and prints `A1 21` correctly (`ffl-text-5.txt:173`, `:255`, `:270`, `:286`).
- job 2: checked candidate-off routing against the shipped loop, including transparent blanks, speaker changes, a carry reopening the answer window, and a drop leaving the previous key intact. The routing branches and emitted sections agree. The copied helpers retain the AFQ behavior apart from the documented measurement adaptations. The informative self-test column and the Key Findings guard example have the discrepancies below.

Findings:

- **PR-MED-201** (MED, replay measurement, `.cursor/loops/ffl_lib.py:635`): `Capture.transcript_call()` selects the reply-credit-off comparison whenever the primary transcript call recursively measures the same note without reply credit. A replay consumer using the returned arguments would measure the wrong baseline on precisely the encounters where credit matters. The Phase 1 text calls pass no reference and do not exercise this path. - Evidence: `for args, kwargs in reversed(self.metric_calls):` selects the last call satisfying `if kwargs.get("reference") is not None:`. The recursive call passes both `reference=reference` and `reply_credit=False` at `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/validation.py:2402–2404`. Recommendation: Fix-now - require a reference and enabled reply credit when selecting the primary transcript call, and verify selection with an outer call followed by its credit-off comparison.
- **PR-LOW-201** (LOW, column interpretation, `.cursor/loops/ffl-measure.py:144`): “x-lines kept” counts encounters whose provider-segment sets differ after finalisation. A displayed zero means every compared set agreed. Reading it as a retained-line count reverses the interpretation. - Evidence: `if lib.provider_segments(scratch.note) != lib.section_segments(` leads to `kept_info += 1`, while `ffl-selftest-3.txt:3` labels the column `x-lines kept (info)`. Recommendation: Fix-now - label it “Encounters with changed provider segment sets” and use that meaning in Task 1.1.
- **PR-LOW-202** (LOW, record consistency, `.cursor/plans/plan-follow-on-lines.md:129`): Key Findings still presents `h4-17` as an example the contiguous guard cannot match. Task 1.2 records the opposite, and the refreshed output confirms it. - Evidence: the plan includes `"don't put the wedding in the letter", `​`h4-17`​`` among examples “which a contiguous phrase cannot match.” `ffl-text-5.txt:173` reads `instruction lines the list matches: h4-17:L5`. Recommendation: Fix-now - keep `h5-08` as the missed example and identify `h4-17` as the matched example in Key Findings.
- **PR-LOW-203** (LOW, set selection, `.cursor/loops/ffl-measure.py:594`): Duplicate set names are accepted. With `--sets heldout,heldout`, the report prints two per-set tables, runs every encounter twice, then reports one set measured because the aggregate dictionary overwrites the first entry. The saved seven-set run uses distinct names. - Evidence: `sets = [name for name in args.sets.split(",") if name]`, `per_set[set_name] = rows` at line 558, and `Sets measured: {len(per_set)}` at line 423. Recommendation: Fix-now - refuse duplicate names or deduplicate in input order before either mode runs.
- **PR-LOW-204** (LOW, data-handling documentation, `.cursor/loops/ffl_lib.py:6`): The library docstring promises that no function returns transcript, script or note text. `text_encounter` returns all three inside `Evaluated`. The distinction matters to the planned reuse of this library for a numbers-only real-speech report. - Evidence: “none returns transcript, script or note text” versus `return Evaluated(script.encounter_id, variant, document, script, note, metrics, plain, record)` at line 775. Recommendation: Fix-now - document which objects carry content in memory and place the counts-only guarantee on the reporting boundary where it applies.

Findings:

- **PR-LOW-301** (LOW, premise verification, `.cursor/loops/ffl-measure.py:632`): A partly missing set can pass Premise 1. If one of the 50 `scripts` encounters is absent, the remaining 49 can produce zero mismatches and exit 0. The saved run includes all 50. - Evidence: `empty = [name for name in sets if not lib.load_scripts(name)]` checks only for zero scripts, and `ffl_lib.py:796` enumerates `glob("*.json")`. - **PR-LOW-302** (LOW, error handling, `.cursor/loops/ffl-measure.py:632`): A malformed or unreadable encounter aborts either mode during preflight, before the runner can issue its content-free diagnostic. - Evidence: `if not lib.load_scripts(name)` invokes `validation.load_script(path)` at `ffl_lib.py:795` outside the exception handlers. Text mode also loads the set at `ffl-measure.py:571` before entering its `try` at line 572. Recommendation: Fix-now - load each selected set once inside a guarded path, report the set and exception type on failure, and return a nonzero status.

An inserted audio phrase with no reference alignment would therefore count as a multi-role segment. - Evidence: `found = {script.lines[line].role for line in lines[segment]}` followed by `roles[next(iter(found)) if len(found) == 1 else "mixed"] += 1`. Recommendation: Fix-now - report `unaligned` for an empty set and reserve `mixed` for two or more distinct roles.
- **PR-LOW-304** (LOW, acceptance criterion, `.cursor/plans/plan-follow-on-lines.md:495`): Task 1.1’s recorded completion cannot satisfy its literal Git criterion. The untracked plan is an expected Phase 1 deliverable. - Evidence: line 495 requires “`git status --short` is empty.” Line 496 records “only `?? .cursor/plans/plan-follow-on-lines.md`”. Findings:

- **PR-MED-401** (MED, measurement artifact integrity, `.cursor/loops/ffl-measure.py:632`): A failed rerun can overwrite complete Task 1.2 tables and JSON projections with partial results. An encounter that raises a trace mismatch is omitted from `rows`. The runner then writes those rows to the regular output paths before returning failure. The JSON carries no indication that an encounter is missing. - Evidence: `rows.append(encounter_row(script, config))` at line 618, `errors += 1` at line 620, `[projection(row, name) for row in rows]` at line 632, and `return 0 if errors == 0 else 1` at line 648. Recommendation: Fix-now - retain the per-encounter diagnostics and publish the tables and projections only after all selected encounters succeed.
- **PR-MED-402** (MED, measurement correctness, `.cursor/loops/ffl_lib.py:1093`): H7 takes the flag difference by transcript segment before counting distinct stay-out script lines. If an audio split leaves one piece of a stay-out line flagged in the baseline and F newly flags a second piece carrying the absent fact, H7 counts the script line as newly flagged even though it was already flagged. The saved text-level totals are unaffected. - Evidence: `extra = sorted(h_flags - b_flags)`, followed by `facts_to_segments.get(fact.fact_index, frozenset()) & extra_set` and `stay_out_lines[line] = stay_out_lines.get(line, False) or fact.material` at lines 1102–1105. Recommendation: Fix-now - map baseline and candidate flags to script-line IDs separately, then take the difference for the stay-out column. Keep the existing segment difference for the extra-flagged-segments count.
- **PR-LOW-401** (LOW, self-test coverage, `.cursor/loops/ffl-measure.py:125`): The round 9 guard check stays green if repeated-ID detection is removed. Its extra-ID case introduces an unknown ID, so it never exercises a duplicate of a valid ID alongside an otherwise complete inventory. The current production check does refuse that input. - Evidence: `lib.inventory_problems("heldout", [*full, "hld-23"]) != ("extra hld-23",)` is the final inventory case, while `ffl_lib.py:822` handles repeats separately with `found.count(i) > 1`. Recommendation: Fix-now - append `full[0]` to the complete fixture and require exactly `("repeated hld-01",)`.

Findings:

- **PR-LOW-601** (LOW, measurement interpretation, `.cursor/loops/ffl_lib.py:1154`): Audio boundary drift can undercount newly flagged stay-out lines as well as overcount them. If a baseline flag on a neighbouring segment aligns spuriously into line 3, the subtraction removes line 3 even when the candidate newly flags line 3’s own segment. The symmetric calculation implements D8, but its result is not a conservative upper bound. - Evidence: `over-count on a harm column, the safe side` and `for lines_of in base_flag_lines.values():` followed by `flagged -= lines_of`. Recommendation: Fix-now - retain the calculation and document both directions of error in the helper and the plan before interpreting audio results.
- **PR-LOW-602** (LOW, measurement artifact integrity, `.cursor/loops/ffl-measure.py:792`): A subset can still write inside the full-run tree. For example, `--sets heldout --out .cursor/loops/ffl-out/text/heldout` passes the resolver and adds a subset summary and nested projections beside the canonical projections. - Evidence: `if not full and _same_path(out, DEFAULT_OUT):` rejects equality only, then `return out, None` accepts the descendant. Recommendation: Fix-now - refuse subset destinations that resolve to the full-run directory or any descendant, and add the nested destination to `_round11_out_ok`.

### Round 16 — 2026-11-19

Source: Codex — codex gpt-6.2-astra medium — Phase 2 code peer review (stage-2.p1, peer round 1)  
Status: Closed (8 applied in leg bx8, verify-then-fix; verified 2026-11-19 by the composer's re-runs `ffl-selftest-14.txt` (0 mismatches; against `ffl-selftest-13.txt` only `seconds:` differs), `ffl-ruff-2.txt` (all checks passed), `ffl-premise2-5.txt` (0 problems, the new Capture column 0 on all nine rows), `ffl-baseline-report-3.txt` (0 problems; `ffl-out/baseline-summary-3.md` byte-equal to `-2`), `ffl-docs-4.txt` (0 problems; round 14's traces moved aside to `-r14` first) and `ffl-cmp-traces.py` (`trace-r14` to `trace` 190 compared, 0 differing; `trace-docs-t2-r14` to `trace-docs-t2` 46 compared, 0 differing; the nine `_set.json` byte-equal); over 227 traces and 4393 segments, 0 segments after the boundary carry a reason and 0 inside it lack one; `h6-16` segment 29 `after_boundary` true; a cue-routed segment's `window` reads the five tests in order)

Scope:

- job 1: checked both provider factories and all D2 comparisons. The scratch replay uses `lib.BASE`, exercises its own routing loop and compares both notes, warnings, outcomes, reference readings, no-credit metrics, credit sets, sections and trace structure. The saved run measured all 227 encounters with zero reported differences. A shared capture failure can bypass part of that check.
- job 2: checked the wrappers against the shipped reference, transcript, nested no-credit and 0.3.4 comparison calls. The wrappers return the original values, restore the functions in `finally`, and pair each judgement with the walk that produced its state. `reduce_judged` independently computes the worst verdict, warning and raw silent flag from captured judgements. All 3,036 fact checks matched.
- job 3: checked the archive comparison, both credit views, retry selection and per-measure floors. Direct comparison of the two `heldout-2` product files found all 233 five-field fact rows equal under each credit. Raw/adjusted silent counts were 4/4 with credit and 19/19 without. The seven-set report contains both required repeat takes and supports its recorded zero floors and no-retry conclusion. Two incomplete-input paths can produce misleading reports.
- job 4: checked segment, note-line, encounter and fact fields against both sample traces and the set summary. The role calculation uses `math.fsum` and the shipped tie tolerance. The saved fields distinguish a mislabelled segment from one crossing the clinician line. `h2-07/f4` maps to segment 8 after the dropped piece at 7. `h6-16/f5` maps to segment 13 with an omission anchor and no segment-level reply or run credit. The trace leaves one D5 guard outcome ambiguous and one boundary convention undocumented.
- job 5: checked the task records and bx1–bx7 handoffs against the permitted outputs. The 6,065-second chain, nine baseline rows, 9,292-second baseline, 183 + 44 traces and 63 text projections agree. Historical smoke exits and comparisons against earlier trace copies are composer-reported evidence. The raw-count statement, invocation count and attribution of the baseline clock times need correction.

Findings:

- **PR-MED-701** (MED, premise verification, `.cursor/loops/ffl-measure.py:993`): A measured encounter can pass Premise 2 when both replays lose the same capture. Two missing primary calls compare equal as `None`. Two missing transcript-note captures also skip the credit, section and trace checks and cause the encounter to be counted as role-unresolved. - Evidence: `if scratch.plain() != shipped.plain():`, `if note is not None:` and `return bad, note is not None` at lines 993–1008. `Replayed.note` returns `None` for `len(self.notes) < 2`, and `plain()` returns `None` for a missing call at `ffl_lib.py:1357–1366`. Recommendation: Fix-now - Derive measurement status from the shipped outcome and require both composed notes and the primary metrics call on each measured replay before comparing values.
- **PR-MED-702** (MED, archive comparison and retry, `.cursor/loops/ffl-measure.py:1543`): One-sided fact IDs disappear from the difference list. A measured-to-unresolved change can therefore print “every fact equal,” and equal-length lists with different IDs can suppress the retry entirely. A completed unresolved retry can also be labelled pending. - Evidence: `for key in sorted(set(mine) & set(theirs)):` and `detail or ["(every fact equal to the archive)"]`. At line 1554 an absent retry fact is passed to `retry_mark`, where `if retry is None: return "retry pending"` also covers a completed retry with an empty fact list. Recommendation: Fix-now - Compare the union of fact IDs, print the missing side explicitly, and distinguish a missing retry take from a missing fact in a completed take.
- **PR-MED-703** (MED, noise-floor verification, `.cursor/loops/ffl-measure.py:1431`): A report can succeed with a mandatory key-set repeat missing. With one repeat present, the missing key set can inherit the other set’s floor. With neither present, the floor table can be empty. - Evidence: `if "docs" not in loaded[set_name]:` checks only take 1. Lines 1573–1574 compute a key-set floor only `if pair is not None`, and line 1613 assigns `others = [name for name in sets if name not in floors]`. Recommendation: Fix-now - Count missing `docs-t2` data for either selected key set as a report problem and exclude that set from inherited floors.
- **PR-MED-704** (MED, D5 cause classification, `.cursor/loops/ffl_lib.py:431`): Task 3.1 cannot reliably decide S3 from the trace when an audio question is interrogative but its answer window stays closed. The trace records the combined window result. If another prerequisite also fails, that result does not reveal whether guard G passed. This matters because D5 specifically tests guard G and assigns the first matching cause. - Evidence: `Reason(index, utterance.speaker, "cued", "window" if opened else "cue", str(key))`. The carried-question path likewise stores `f"{test}+window" if opened else test` at line 480. The shipped helper returns early for speaker, suspect-word and section checks before `return self._question_routes_alone(request, speaker, joined, key)` at `note.py:2328–2337`. Recommendation: Fix-now - Record the guard-G result for the actual question words, including joined cue-and-carry pieces, separately from whether the window opened.
- **PR-LOW-701** (LOW, trace contract, `.cursor/plans/plan-follow-on-lines.md:744`): Task 2.3 promises a reason code per segment, but segments outside the detected boundary have no provider reason. The distinction matters to a reader classifying a next-patient tail. - Evidence: `h6-16.json:107` records `"transcript": 29`, and segment 29 at lines 2443–2470 has `"reason": null`. The generator writes `None if reason is None` at `ffl_lib.py:1981–1983`. Recommendation: Fix-now - Document the post-boundary null convention for audio and omitted reason rows for text, or emit an explicit outside-boundary value.
- **PR-LOW-702** (LOW, baseline record, `.cursor/plans/plan-follow-on-lines.md:122`): Key Findings says archived adjusted silent counts equal raw counts throughout. The `scripts` set differs under both credits. - Evidence: “on these files it equals the raw count”; `baseline-summary-2.md` gives `| archive (0.3.7) | QT | 50 | 1 | 0 |` and the same raw 1 / adjusted 0 for `shipped`. Recommendation: Fix-now - Correct the parenthesis to name the `scripts` exception already recorded in Task 2.2.
- **PR-LOW-703** (LOW, capture coverage record, `.cursor/plans/plan-follow-on-lines.md:666`): Round 13 multiplies the encounter total by the number of set/take rows and describes the product as metrics invocations. - Evidence: “over 227 × 9 invocations.” The nine rows in `ffl-premise2-4.txt` already sum to 227 encounters. Each measured replay has a reference and transcript call, with a conditional nested no-credit call at `validation.py:2398`. Recommendation: Fix-now - Record 227 encounters across nine set/take rows and use the independently reported 3,036 figure for fact checks.
- **PR-LOW-704** (LOW, timing provenance, `.cursor/plans/plan-follow-on-lines.md:727`): The Task 2.2 record attributes exact start and finish times to a log that records elapsed time only. The duration is verified. - Evidence: “`ffl-baseline-1.txt` (2026-11-18T23:47 → 2026-11-19T02:22, 9292 s, HEAD `60cd138`)”. The log opens with the configuration and HEAD and ends `seconds: 9292`; the timer is `time.monotonic()` at `ffl-measure.py:1229`. Recommendation: Fix-now - Attribute the clock times to the composer’s observation or retain only the logged duration.

Findings:

- **PR-MED-901** (MED, trace completeness, `.cursor/loops/ffl_lib.py:2040`): A missing text-level provider record produces a successful trace with no text routing reasons. Task 3.1 then lacks evidence for S3, S4 and S7, while the encounter still counts as written. - Evidence: `record = log.get(transcript_digest(document))` at line 840 can return `None`. `build_trace` substitutes `[] if text_record is None`. The independent text replay starts at `ffl-measure.py:1784`, after the capture-loss check, and line 1797 executes `written += 1`. - **PR-LOW-901** (LOW, report input validation, `.cursor/loops/ffl-measure.py:1926`): The subset refusal reads the selected scripts and note configuration first. An unreadable input can mask the D4 refusal, contrary to the round 17 statement that it “precedes every read.” - Evidence: `loaded, refusal = load_sets(sets)` and `config = load_note_config(args.config)` precede `if sorted(sets) != sorted(lib.SEEN_SETS):` at line 1942. - **PR-LOW-903** (LOW, report overwrite, `.cursor/loops/ffl-measure.py:1657`): Two report invocations targeting the same fresh summary can both pass the existence check and the later finisher can overwrite the first report. - Evidence: `if args.out is None or args.out.exists():` at line 1945 runs before the report reads its inputs. Findings:

- **PR-MED-1001** (MED, measurement reporting, `.cursor/loops/ffl-measure.py:1543`): The report omits the shipped raw `material_wrong` count. Its `wrong`-verdict column is valid for reproducing Key Findings, but it excludes material `wrongly_present` facts that enter the adjacent adjusted measure. A reader cannot determine how much the residue adjustment removed. - Evidence: The raw expression is `if f[1] and f[2] == 'wrong'` at lines 1528 and 1543. The shipped predicate is `self.material and self.verdict in ("wrong", "wrongly_present")` at `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/validation.py:979`. The baseline already saves `"material_wrong": metrics.material_wrong` at `.cursor/loops/ffl_lib.py:1668`. Recommendation: Fix-now - Keep the `wrong`-only column and print raw `material_wrong` beside `material_wrong_v2` for both credits, deriving the archive’s raw value from both verdicts.
- **PR-LOW-1001** (LOW, archive error handling, `.cursor/loops/ffl-measure.py:1523`): A malformed archive row can still abort the entire report before it prints the run and reduction results. A JSON row with valid encounter and credit keys but no `counts` passes the guarded load. - Evidence: Line 1347 indexes only `r["encounter"], r["credit"]`. Line 1523 subsequently reads `row["counts"]` outside the handler, and printing begins at line 1667. Recommendation: Fix-now - Validate the consumed archive row, count and fact fields inside the per-set error boundary and report the affected set.
- **PR-LOW-1002** (LOW, noise-floor reporting, `.cursor/loops/ffl-measure.py:1514`): A caught archive error also discards that set’s available take counts and noise floors. If the set supplied the largest floor, the inherited maximum can be too small. The retry footer can also say `none` when the comparison was unavailable. - Evidence: The handler says `"its archive comparison and floors are skipped"` and executes `continue`. Line 1610 selects `pair = take2 if set_name in lib.KEY_SETS else retry` independently of the archive, while line 1663 prints `', '.join(retry_needed) or 'none'`. Recommendation: Fix-now - Keep computing take-only counts and floors, and mark the affected set’s retry decision as unknown.
- **PR-LOW-1003** (LOW, report input dependencies, `.cursor/loops/ffl-measure.py:1962`): A full seven-set report still depends on readable scripts and note configuration even though it consumes saved measurements. A missing config prevents regeneration from otherwise complete baseline and archive files. - Evidence: `loaded, refusal = load_sets(sets)` and `config = load_note_config(args.config)` precede `return baseline_report(sets, args.baseline_dir, args.out)` at line 1979. Neither `loaded` nor `config` is passed to the report. Recommendation: Fix-now - Dispatch full report mode after set-name and output validation, before loading scripts or note configuration.
- **PR-LOW-1004** (LOW, concurrent output custody, `.cursor/loops/ffl-chain.sh:25`): Two chain invocations can both pass the fresh-root check and start writing to the same built-set tree. Distinct run numbers protect neither the shared root nor its transcripts. - Evidence: Line 25 tests `if [ -e "$ROOT" ]` without reserving the directory. Both processes can then reach line 47 and build into `"$ROOT/$s"`. With the same run number, line 33 also opens `"$L/ffl-c$N-voices.txt"` with truncation. Recommendation: Fix-now - Claim the fresh root with an exclusive directory creation before opening step logs or starting builds.
- **PR-LOW-1005** (LOW, timing record, `.cursor/plans/plan-follow-on-lines.md:923`): The seven builds took 153 seconds. The recorded 154 seconds includes the voice check. - Evidence: The task says “154 s in all.” `.cursor/loops/ffl-chain.out:5` says `[voices] baseline OK; took 1 s`, and line 20 says `took 154 s since start`. The seven logged build durations sum to 153. Recommendation: Fix-now - Record 153 seconds for the builds and 154 seconds elapsed since chain start.
- **PR-LOW-1006** (LOW, output locator, `.cursor/plans/plan-follow-on-lines.md:159`): D3 directs readers to `L/ffl-out/` for the run logs, while the chain and step logs are directly under `L/`. - Evidence: D3 says “logs, traces and tables under `L/ffl-out/`.” `.cursor/loops/ffl-chain.sh:20` sets `L=.cursor/loops`, and line 33 writes `"$L/ffl-c$N-voices.txt"`. Recommendation: Fix-now - Identify `L/` as the log directory and `L/ffl-out/` as the directory for structured outputs.

The baseline took 9,292 seconds, and the text run produced 63 projections. The historical trace comparisons and summary-5 byte comparison are supported by the composer’s closing statuses. No additional record discrepancy was found.

Findings:

- **PR-LOW-1201** (LOW, archive error handling, `.cursor/loops/ffl-measure.py:1377`): A null warned or adjusted-silent flag passes archive validation and aborts the report when the differing fact is printed. A numeric encounter ID can likewise reach a mixed-type sort outside the per-set handler. - Evidence: The fact check ends with `and isinstance(f[2], str)`. Line 1409 formats `w{int(fact[3])} s{int(fact[4])}`, and line 1636 sorts the union of archive and take encounter IDs. Recommendation: Fix-now - Validate both flags and the encounter identifier inside `load_archive` so malformed values take the existing per-set error path.
- **PR-LOW-1202** (LOW, noise-floor integrity, `.cursor/loops/ffl-measure.py:1519`): A take-2 `product.json` missing one encounter’s `QT` row can yield a successful report with an inflated fact floor. The payload still supplies that encounter to the reduction check, while the floor treats its missing product facts as differences between takes. The inflated floor can propagate to all five sets that inherit the maximum. - Evidence: Line 1519 checks `if len(ts.payloads) != run["encounters"]:`. Line 1700 calculates `lib.fact_floor(take1.facts("QT"), pair.facts("QT"))`, and `.cursor/loops/ffl_lib.py:1693` counts `left is None or right is None` as a difference. Recommendation: Fix-now - Require exactly one `QT` row and one `shipped` row per payload encounter before using a take for floor calculations. A genuinely unmeasured encounter already has explicit rows with empty fact lists.

Verification: 33 distinct scoped files read, 6 of 6 jobs evidenced. The other six archive comparisons were checked through the saved report. No scripts or suites were run and no files were written.

On take 1, A1 reached one fact and added 42 lines, A1s reached one and added 32, and F-credit reached four and added none. Both key sets had zero base fact differences between takes under either credit. The recorded run used the prescribed takes.
- job 4: checked dispatch, refusal paths, publication, default rendering parameters, and the four refusal logs. Both modes refuse existing destinations and subset runs. The text run reports zero errors and 63 projections. Its log records generation; byte equality with the earlier projections is the composer’s separately recorded comparison. Exclusive creation prevents overwrite, but an interrupted write can leave a partial file.
- job 5: checked Task 3.1, Task 3.2, and the cx1–cx5 handoffs against the historical and current maps, audio tables, baseline, and logs. The principal counts and seed results agree. The record overstates what zero guard refusals establishes, miscounts the set-level rows in two places, and cites the wrong task for one comparison.

Findings:

- **PR-MED-1501** (MED, classifier correctness, `.cursor/loops/ffl_lib.py:2336`): The note-origin tests discard unquoted fact segments before testing agreement. A fact spanning one suggestion-quoted segment and one unquoted segment can receive W6 outright, preempting W3 and losing the mixed result. The same helper serves W4, W1, N3, P4, and P2. - Evidence: `covered = self.covered(segments)` followed by `for s in covered`; D5 requires agreement “for every segment of the set” (`.cursor/plans/plan-follow-on-lines.md:162`). Recommendation: Fix-now - Evaluate every aligned fact segment, count an unquoted segment as false, and exercise a quoted/unquoted pair before regenerating the map.
- **PR-MED-1502** (MED, missing report column, `.cursor/loops/ffl_lib.py:2531`): The 29 wrong and wrongly-present rows omit D5’s independent “silent at text level too” flag. This matters on the current map: `h2-15/f9` and `h6-14/f5` both print `omitted`, which leaves the reader unable to tell whether the omission was warned. - Evidence: `str(text_row[_ROW_VERDICT])` is the only text result emitted for these rows; the shipped metric separately computes `silently_omitted_somewhere=any(...)` (`desktop/src/scribe_desktop/validation.py:2302`). Recommendation: Fix-now - Print the raw text-silent flag for every fact row and retain the verdict in its own column.
- **PR-MED-1503** (MED, reconciliation integrity, `.cursor/loops/ffl-measure.py:2794`): A missing archived seed can produce a successful map with an incomplete reconciliation. A missing take-1 seed can also be described as “no longer failing on take 1” without a measured state establishing that conclusion. All 13 states are present in the current record. - Evidence: `archived = next((f for f in archived_row["facts"] if f[0] == index), None)`; the take-1 lookup also defaults to `None`, and `_five` renders it as `"(none)"` without recording a problem. Recommendation: Fix-now - Require exactly one archive state and one take-1 state per registered seed before publishing.
- **PR-MED-1504** (MED, set population integrity, `.cursor/loops/ffl_lib.py:2615`): A missing or duplicated residue-class entry can silently change the failure population. For example, omitting heldout-2’s R2 entry drops its 9/1 breach while the ranked sum still equals the number of rows constructed. - Evidence: `for count in set_classes_json["classes"]["gate"]["QT"]:` followed by `if not count["within"]:` accepts the entries supplied without checking that R1, R2, and R3 each occur once. The current breach is visible at `.cursor/loops/ffl-out/map-2.md:100`. Recommendation: Fix-now - Validate class identities, totals, and ceilings against the pinned rule before accepting each set.
- **PR-MED-1505** (MED, unsupported routing conclusion, `.cursor/plans/plan-follow-on-lines.md:1246`): Zero audio refusals does not establish where `hld-17`’s text-level off-record line went. A lost or split phrase can prevent a match, and a cue route bypasses this guard. The audio table reports A1 adding `hld-17:s3` and `hld-17:s9` without identifying either as the script line in question. - Evidence: The record concludes “so at audio no path places that line”; the actual condition is `refused = variant.guard and holds_off_record(utterance.text)` followed by `if refused and (hop is None or carry is None or follow_on):` (`.cursor/loops/ffl_lib.py:479`). Recommendation: Fix-now - Record the observed zero refusals. Retain a line-placement conclusion only if its audio alignment and routing reason establish it.
- **PR-LOW-1501** (LOW, classifier correctness, `.cursor/loops/ffl_lib.py:2416`): A segment excluded by the transcript boundary can satisfy S7, attributing the failure to a misheard cue even though the router never examined that segment. No current map row is S7. - Evidence: `view.segments[s]["reason"] is None or view.segments[s]["reason"]["code"] != "cued"`; the trace builder says a segment after the boundary “is never in the request, so its reason is None” (`.cursor/loops/ffl_lib.py:2022`). Recommendation: Fix-now - Require an observed routing decision for S7 and add a boundary-excluded case to the classifier check.
- **PR-LOW-1502** (LOW, output integrity, `.cursor/loops/ffl-measure.py:2570`): Both modes create the final pathname before the report has finished writing. An interruption or write failure can leave an empty or truncated record that the next run refuses to replace. - Evidence: `with out.open("x", encoding="utf-8", newline="\n") as handle:` precedes `handle.write(...)` in both the audio and map paths (`.cursor/loops/ffl-measure.py:2828`); only `FileExistsError` is handled. Recommendation: Fix-now - Finish and close a sibling temporary file, then publish it with a no-overwrite rename and clean up on failure.
- **PR-LOW-1503** (LOW, take selection, `.cursor/loops/ffl-measure.py:2974`): `--audio` accepts an extra `docs-t3` alongside the required takes and can publish candidate tables for a retry reserved for baseline comparison. - Evidence: `plan, refusal = premise2_plan(args.take, sets)` feeds the whole plan to `audio_mode`; the shared selector explicitly accepts `if take == "docs-t3":` (`.cursor/loops/ffl_lib.py:1371`). Recommendation: Fix-now - Require exactly `docs` and `docs-t2` for audio mode before replay.
- **PR-LOW-1504** (LOW, rule interpretation, `.cursor/loops/ffl_lib.py:2143`): The classifier commentary and Task 3.1 incorrectly identify N2 as an R3 case. N2 requires a boundary found late, whereas shipped R3 requires an absent transcript boundary. The current counted column uses the shipped residue flag. - Evidence: “D5’s classes N1/N2 are R3’s own cases”; N2 checks `found is not None` (`.cursor/loops/ffl_lib.py:2476`), and shipped R3 checks `and boundary is None` (`desktop/src/scribe_desktop/validation.py:2349`). Recommendation: Fix-now - Correct the explanation in the classifier and Task 3.1 while preserving raw wrongly-present rows.
- **PR-LOW-1505** (LOW, record count, `.cursor/plans/plan-follow-on-lines.md:1232`): Task 3.1 and leg cx2 call seven over-ceiling set/class rows six. Six is the number of affected sets. - Evidence: “The six rows come from `_set.json`”; heldout-3 contributes both `R1 | 2 | 1 | OVER` and `R3 | 2 | 1 | OVER` (`.cursor/loops/ffl-out/map-2.md:102` and `:104`), and the predicate total is `set 7` (`:142`). Recommendation: Fix-now - Use “seven rows across six sets” in both records.
- **PR-LOW-1506** (LOW, handoff locator, `.cursor/plans/plan-follow-on-lines.md:347`): Leg cx2 sends the composer to Task 3.1 for off-configuration baseline counts. Task 3.1 contains the gate-configuration map; the off-configuration base is measured by Task 3.2. - Evidence: “must read Task 3.1’s off-configuration counts” conflicts with the map’s opening description, “the gate configuration, the reply credit on” (`.cursor/loops/ffl-out/map-2.md:1`). Recommendation: Fix-now - Identify Task 2.2’s gate counts as the cross-configuration comparison and Task 3.2’s base rows as the off-configuration measurement.
